ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u13	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the reasons for the Government's legislative initiative to amend the Local Tax and Charges Act, print No 1729. This initiative in the form of an amendment to the Act on Local Taxes and Charges is due to the demands raised by local government units to seal the system of real estate tax exemptions for railway property in connection with the use of railway land for activities in an area which is not related to rail. This is directly related to the judicial practice of the courts. [[Game in the O.R.]] [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]] Minister, just a moment, please. I ask your Members to silence the talks, possibly to move them outside the Parliamentary Chamber, because we are preventing the Minister from speaking. I welcome Senator Zygmunt Frankiewicz, who will justify the bill. Senator, you're welcome. Just a moment, Minister. I'll let you know. Senator, take your seat. Maybe not in the president's chair. You're welcome, Minister. Secretary of State of the Ministry of Infrastructure Andrzej Bittel: Thank you very much. The sealing project is directly linked to the judicial practice of the administrative courts, which, in my opinion, accept an extension of the interpretation of the current rules. The project clarifies the scope of the real estate tax exemption in the area of rail real estate exemption in order to seal the exemption system and comprehensive regulation of the area. The proposed real estate tax exemptions include land, buildings and buildings that are part of a railway infrastructure or service facility, within the meaning of the Railway Transport Act, in part exclusively occupied for the performance of the infrastructure manager's tasks or for the provision of services by the service facility operator, where the railway infrastructure or service facility is made available to railway operators or railway infrastructure or service facility is used for passenger transport, or the railway infrastructure creates wide-range railway lines, or the railway infrastructure is a closed infrastructure. The second point on this exemption covers land, buildings and buildings in the part intended for the provision by the railway operator or by the operator of a passenger station of services directly related to the service of travellers. The third part is property other than those referred to above, constituting a railway area, with the exception of land, buildings and structures used for non-rail activities. This requirement to seal the system boils down to the fact that the property is exempt from real estate tax only in the part in which it is engaged in the operation of railway activities within the areas indicated just now, by linking the exemption to the operation of railway activities consisting in the management of railway infrastructure, service facility, the provision of services related to the direct service of travellers, the use of infrastructure or facilities for the transport of passengers. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that before the implementation in 2016 of Directive 34 on the establishment of a single European railway area in the Polish legal order, rail infrastructure constituted all railway elements constituting a technical and utility whole, enabling the carriage of persons or goods. Together with the implementation of the above. the Directive has changed the nomenclature, which has resulted in a distinction between railway infrastructure, covering in principle lines and railway sidings, and service infrastructure facilities, including infrastructure components for inter alia. the assembly of trains or manoeuvring and transhipments, as well as passenger stations, technical facilities, parking tracks and other installations necessary for the smooth and safe operation of rail transport. It is therefore important that all elements enabling the transport of goods or persons on a functional basis should be exempted. For this reason, rail infrastructure and service facilities were exempted from real estate tax. The exemption is also proposed to cover wide-range railway lines, similar to the existing legal situation. The inclusion of a tax exemption for inactive infrastructure is justified in view of the need to maintain this infrastructure, as well as the fact that the tax exemption would not benefit from the infrastructure exempted from traffic in order to modernise it. In accordance with the provisions of the Railway Transport Act, the infrastructure in which the infrastructure manager did not allow traffic should be understood as closed infrastructure. Literally this means that it will be an infrastructure managed by an entity which has the status of an infrastructure manager on which there is no traffic. The exemption for parts of real estate intended for the provision of services directly related to the service of travellers aims to clarify the exemption from property tax for those elements of passenger infrastructure which are necessary to maintain in view of public expectations. First, the proposed exemption is conditional on the provision of services by the railway operator or the operator of the passenger station. Secondly, the exemption will cover only part of the real estate occupied for the provision of services related to direct travel services. And thirdly, the concept of service directly related services has not raised any doubts so far. However, in order to avoid differences in interpretation, the reasons for the proposed act are set out in the elements related to the direct handling of travellers. The elements subject to the tax exemption include public areas directly linked to the handling of passengers by the railway or passenger station operator, including waiting rooms, station halls and mezzanines, separate spaces adapted for feeding and scrolling children, toilets, access to green areas, e.g. for passengers travelling with animals, areas in front of ticket offices, areas occupied by ticket offices and other areas intended for sale of tickets, information points, pedestrian traffic lines, occupied areas under passenger lifts and other equipment for the movement of persons with reduced mobility, ramps, footbridges, shelters against atmospheric factors, parking and parking spaces for vehicles, public roads to platforms, railway station or other public spaces, access lines to parking spaces with infrastructure - freight cranes, intruders, ramps, viaducts, tunnels - areas for placing additional information for passengers designated by the operator of the passenger station in accordance with applicable regulations, technical facilities in the section serving the proper functioning of the above. passenger station elements. Ww. the elements and surfaces are intended to provide adequate, i.e. universal and general, accessibility of passenger service facilities for travellers using them and thus serve the provision of a service directly related to the service of travellers, i.e. a service to meet the needs of travellers directly related to the purpose of the rail transport journey. Examples include: parking and parking spaces for vehicles made available for public transport passengers, such as Park & Ride parking lots, Bike & Ride stations, for travelers from another means of transport: car, motorcycle, bike, scooter etc. To the train. This solution is also intended to enable passenger stations to be converted into transport hubs linking different branches and modes of transport: cars with rail, long-distance and urban bike with rail - different elements can be introduced here. As regards the last issue I wanted to raise, namely the exemption for other non-commercial real estate - these are, as a general rule, non-rail use - the tax exemption for land, buildings and buildings located in a railway area which are not used for other than rail activities is justified in order to maintain the existing tax exemption for, among others. railway land such as intersector or safety belts, as well as immovable property held as a reserve for future needs of the railway sector, including investment. Without this footnote, these properties would be subject to property tax due to the wording of the provision sealing the system, since the previously indicated tax exemption concerns only part of the property intended to perform the tasks of the infrastructure manager or infrastructure operator. I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u15	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate proposes that you adopt a short law that eliminates the obvious tax gap in real estate tax, which is the income of municipalities. The amendment of the Railway Transport Act of 16 November 2016 amended Article. Paragraph 7 1 Act on local taxes and charges. It resulted in the exemption of all registration parcels on which the railway infrastructure was located, even if only small parts of those parcels were earmarked for this purpose, and the main activity of the operator was not related to the railway activity. As a result, there have been very significant losses in tax revenues and illegal public aid. Self-governments have repeatedly intervened in this matter practically from the beginning, i.e. for 5 years. The sum of losses, which is quite difficult to determine, by local governments is estimated at over PLN 1 billion during this period, which is really significant money. If we consider that this tax is not lost evenly by all municipalities, but on a very large scale by a few, this leads to the conclusion that some municipalities are in a very bad financial situation. The Senate's proposal seeks to amend the wording of the Article. Paragraph 7 Point 1, namely the addition of the wording "excluding buildings, buildings and land used for purposes other than those laid down in the Rail Transport Act'. There's a similar record in the government project. These two draft laws in this section are very similar, but the government project is wider. Namely, it extends the exemption as regards the service infrastructure used for the provision of passenger services and rail traffic services, and there are reservations that the service can only be related to the task of the infrastructure manager or to the provision of services by the operator within the meaning of the Railway Transport Act. These safeguards seem sufficient, but there is concern about the effectiveness of these records, because we have experience from five years ago, when it also seemed that the bill, colloquially speaking, is tight and created a tax gap. The second extension is the new extension to a closed infrastructure with an unlimited exemption from real estate tax of this inactive infrastructure. I don't think that's a good solution. It is stated in the explanatory memorandum that the reason for this exemption is to carry out modernisations and renovations, which are by nature temporary in nature, and therefore, in my view, the exemption for an unlimited period of time is a mistake. The property tax has not only a fiscal function, it also leads to active management of real estate. When the exemption is unlimited, there will be no significant incentive for active management of this infrastructure, and this will lead even more to the maintenance, often in city centres, of neglected infrastructure, which is a fatal business card. That's what we should leave. And perhaps the most important issue, namely in the government project the proposed date of entry into force of the Act is 1 January 2023, i.e. the 5 years of the existence of losses in the income of local government units will be the whole year. The Senate printing was accepted in the Senate even a year ago, also on November 16th. There was time to deal with this so that the amended provision would enter into force on 1 January 2022. That is what I am asking, and I am asking very much, because, as I said, the problem may not apply to very many local authorities, but to those concerned in a dramatic way. These are often small municipalities that do not have diversified sources of income, and the loss in this area is extremely painful for them. I'm just going to name some examples of communities like Terespol. Or the Fame. The larger municipalities are also concerned, but there is a wider tax base. Among such larger cities is Auschwitz or Swarzędzim. I think that in the mobilisation of our side, i.e. the government side, and the Sejm, and the Senate, it is still possible to publish in November and enter into force the rule from 1 January 2022. I believe that abuse in this area can no longer be tolerated because the problem is growing from one year to the next. Namely, cases of land consolidation are known in order to ensure that in a large area where only a small part is occupied under railway infrastructure, and that large economic activities outside the railway sector are carried out, there is a exemption from property tax. This is an unjustified state aid and an entirely unacceptable loss in the income of local government units. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u16	Thank you very much, senator. I encourage you to stay and take part in the discussion that will take place in the Parliament. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u17	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government's draft law on amending the Act on Local Taxes and Charges, print No. 1729, and against the Senate draft law, print No. 919. The project concerns the clarification of the scope of the real estate tax exemption as regards railway land, in particular land occupied by railway infrastructure managers and service infrastructure operators. Designed solutions seal the system of real estate tax exemptions when using railway land to operate in an area not related to rail. As a consequence, the proposed solutions will eliminate the abuse of interpretation of the current rules by some entrepreneurs who, while not operating on the railway market, use the provision to avoid taxation and reduce the revenue from property tax to municipal budgets. The real estate tax exemptions for railway infrastructure have been introduced since the beginning of the Act on local taxes and charges. Until today, tax exemptions are still identical and include railway land. But because of the implementation of the European Union's legal system in Poland, the nomenclature has changed. Therefore, what was still a railway infrastructure six years ago may now be a service infrastructure operating and operating within the rail system harmonised by European Union law. The proposed provision will allow for taxing parts of land, buildings or buildings used for commercial activities, i.e. e.g. leased, rented by entities other than the railway operator or the operator of the passenger station. The proposed law also adds a new point to reduce the burden on real estate tax for land, buildings and buildings which are not used for any activity and are maintained as a reserve of land, buildings and buildings for the development of the railway sector, including future investments. At the same time, an exception is introduced in which the existence of an economic activity other than rail will result in the absence of a real estate tax exemption. The entry into force of these regulations should result in an increase in tax revenue to some of the municipalities' budgets due to the sealing of the use of tax exemptions in the railway sector. The proposed solutions aim to eliminate the problem raised by local government units regarding tax avoidance by entities engaged in other than rail economic activities. The date of entry into force of the proposed law was set at 1 January 2023 due to the need to maintain the corresponding vacathio legis for the proposed regulations. This solution will also allow the examination of new regulations and appropriate procedures to be carried out in the context of possible State aid in relation to freight terminals. It is worth noting that in the Sejm a senate draft law on amending the Act on Local Taxes and Charges was submitted, the Sejm No. 919, which deals with the abuse of real estate tax exemptions, causing the risk of further use of exemptions by non-businesses operating on the railway market. At the same time, the Senate project would lead to almost double taxation of railway entities compared to the current one, which would lead in fact to subsidising local government units by these entities and to halting the development of rail transport in Poland. Therefore, the government project deserves to be adopted as soon as possible, because on the one hand it seals the tax system and on the other it promotes rail transport and its development as a safe and environmentally sound means of transport for both people and goods. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to say that the club will be working on both draft laws on amending the Law on railway taxes and charges, print Nos 1729 and 919. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u18	Thank you very much for your position. The Civic Coalition will now speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club by Mrs Zofia Czernow.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u19	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club of the Civic Coalition on draft laws amending the Act on Local Taxes and Charges: the Senate draft law contained in print No. 919 and the government draft law contained in print No. 1729. The purpose of these draft laws is to clarify the scope of the real estate tax exemption set out in Article. 7 Act on local taxes and charges in the area of exemption of railway land. Currently, there is a tax gap to avoid taxation by entities operating on a railway site other than railway business. These are, for example, mines, shopping malls, warehouses, garages and other types of highly commercial business. There are municipalities here - I will mention, for example, those from my district - like Osiecznica, a municipality that has mainly forests, Nowogrodziec, but also Terespol, Sławków and a number of others on its surface. For this reason, local authorities are suffering heavy losses in respect of property tax, which is estimated at about PLN 200 million per year. The Senate draft law clarifies and narrows the rules in the article. 7, which defines the scope of the real estate tax exemption. The project introduces a solution whereby the exemption will not apply to the entire registration parcel, as it is currently referred to as a railway parcel, but only to the part where the elements of the railway infrastructure which are used for the purposes set out in the Railway Transport Act are located. It should be stressed that the Senate project, sealing the property tax and very expected by local authorities, entered the Sejm even a year ago and waited all year long for the procession. The question arises: Why is it taking so long? The government project contains rules that also close the tax gap. In this part of the project, these projects are convergent and do not raise doubts. How do these projects differ? First and foremost, the date of entry into force of the expected changes. The government project proposes the entry into force of 1 January 2023, mainly because of the avoidance of a plea of breach of the constitutional rule of the democratic rule of law. In line with the decisions of the Constitutional Court of 2001 and 2005, changes in taxes the tax dimension of which is made for an annual period should be announced at least one month before the end of the previous tax year. Questions arise. Why did the government wait so long on its project until there was a danger of exceeding the deadline one month before the end of the tax year? Why didn't the Marshal of the Sejm refer to the Senate project, which sealed the property tax? And yet tax sealing is a top priority for the government. Will local government lose another 200 million PLN in 2022 for the above reasons? I hope that the government and the Sejm will make good use of this short but still possible time for the Act to enter into force on 1 January 2022. And the other difference. The government project introduces additional reliefs in real estate tax, i.e. exemption from so-called tax. the railway-related service infrastructure, the permanent exemption of inactive infrastructure, which is temporarily excluded due to planned renovation and modernisation, the exemption of land which constitute provisions for railway development. These proposals for additional reductions raise doubts, and in particular the permanent release of inactive infrastructure, which is to be temporarily excluded according to the project. In our view, this time of release should be precisely defined and adopted e.g. The most appropriate 5-year release time. So far it's been -- I remind you -- three years. I hope that the doubts regarding additional reliefs and the constructive establishment of vacathio legis will be carefully addressed, and that the projects will be directed to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u27	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! Mr. Senator! High Izbo! The proposed laws amend the Act of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges. The main objective of the projects presented is to clarify the provisions determining the scope of the real estate tax exemption referred to in Article. Paragraph 7 1 of this law in the section on exemption from rail land tax. The proposed solutions remove the real estate tax gap, which results in the application of the real estate tax exemption to entities that have in fact nothing to do with railway infrastructure activities other than the fact that they operate on railway land. This is, of course, the result of the statutory tax exemption for parcels registered as railway areas. The current wording does not distinguish whether there are elements of railway infrastructure on the plot or whether there are other activities. Such a systematics, of course, has negative consequences. First, it favours some of the operators operating on railway land by applying the real estate tax exemption. This leads to an unjustified competitive advantage over those entrepreneurs who operate on other land and normally pay property tax. Secondly, it also means losing part of the income of the local government, i.e. the inhabitants of those places where the land is exempt from tax. The result of this situation is e.g. lower investment expenditure. As the senator pointed out, the sum of these redundancies could already have reached over PLN 1 billion, which is a very large amount of money. With regard to both projects, it should be noted that in order to clarify the content of the provision in Article. 7 (1) Senate and Government projects are similar, while the government project is thus extending the exemption to include those infrastructure elements which serve to provide services related to the provision of travel services and to inactive infrastructure. Here, of course, the question arises as to how these exemptions will be applied, because there is no justification for the exemption to be subject to those parts which constitute, for example, an exemption. elements for commercial activities. On behalf of the Parliamentary Circle, Poland 2050 requests that the project be submitted to the committee for further work. Doubts concerning (the bell) even the part used for commercial activities must be settled. There is no justification for such land being exempt. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u119	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government's draft law amending the Social Assistance Act, print no. 1672 and 1745. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 21 October 2021, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for first reading. The Commission for Social Policy and Family Policy carried out its first reading and examined the draft law at its meeting on 15 November 2021. The purpose of this regulation is to extend the catalogue of conditions for exempting family members of the resident of the house from payment for his stay in that house and results from the social need to guarantee greater protection for family members of the pensioners of those institutions. The aim of the project is to eliminate the sense of injustice towards family members of persons who have grossly failed to fulfil their parental responsibilities. The Act sets out both optional and mandatory conditions in a precise manner. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, after considering the draft at its meeting on 15 November 2021, requests the High House to adopt a law in accordance with print No 1672. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u123	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Coalition club on the government bill amending the Social Assistance Act, prints no. 1672 and 1745. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to guarantee greater protection for families whose relatives are in social assistance homes by extending the catalogue of mandatory conditions and optional dismissal of families of those residents of the DPS for payment for their stay in social assistance units. According to current regulations, first of all, the resident should pay for his stay in the DPS. It is an amount of up to 70%. income. If this is not possible, the spouse, the relative of the alternate before the initial, and if there are no relatives or the funds due from them cannot be enforced, the stay shall be paid by the municipality from which the person was sent to the DPS. The exemption from payment for stays at a social assistance home is provided by Article. 64 Social Services Act. This article indicates that a person who has been in a foster family, a family home or a nursing home on the basis of a court decision to restrict parental authority to a person addressed to a DPS or a resident of the home may be exempt from payment. These changes introduced a few years ago are as fair as possible and in practice they work. However, as is apparent from the justification and from the consultation that I carried out with social workers of social assistance centres and representatives of social assistance homes, the problem arises when the parent has not been deprived of parental rights in due time. This is the case when, at the most difficult time of lack of care and the basic needs of a child, the second parent or guardian who experiences violence himself, in order to ensure the safety of children, will not lead to the end of the procedure of deprivation of parental rights, will not see to it. Thus, he will not receive a final judgment on the deprivation of parental authority. In addition to the bad news, the second parent is dead, which in practice eliminates the possibility of depriving the sole guardian of abuse. The social assistance environment is consistent with the need to clarify these issues in order to ensure that the institution of the exemption also covers cases of parental proceedings which are described as contrary to the principles of social life. The introduction into the Social Assistance Act of a clear legal framework allowing the competent authority to waive payment for a stay at a social assistance home is expected and justified. The project that we are investigating today provides for the possibility of exempting a person from the DPS residence fee for whom he has grossly neglected parental responsibilities in the past, leading to the cessation of family ties, and when it was a gross violation by the pensioner of maintenance obligations towards the child or neglecting other family responsibilities, e.g. duty to care, raise or maintain. This optional condition will justify a DPS resident's refusal to finance. In addition, the project extends the mandatory conditions. In addition to violent crimes, they were introduced by drunkenness or abandonment of a minor by a parent or guardian. Adult children have a responsibility to care for their parents. For many of us it is obvious and we do not imagine it could be different. However, this principle cannot be used and abused by people who are only formally parents, and in fact have never taken care of their children and have not been financially involved in their maintenance. You often hear about cases of adults already children who, after decades, learn that a father who was a violent person or a mother who abandoned children has remembered their existence and is required to support them for their stay in a social security facility. I think many of us know different stories, like the one described by a woman. I received a reply from the SKO on the maintenance of the contested decision to pay for the father who was in the DPS. I don't know what I should do right now. A while ago, I got a decision from OPS that I had to pay the fees I had to cancel. Meanwhile, my father died. Of course, I had no contact with my father. He wasn't interested in me at all. He didn't pay his alimony. I was in foster care, but because my mom was schizophrenic, she wasn't able to deprive him of his parental rights. SKO claims that there are no negative emotional ties with the father, and the party has a legal obligation to pay the fee. Furthermore, it is noted that the exemption procedure can only be initiated when the decision setting the amount of the fee becomes final. I have no idea what to do in this situation. These things are human dramas and rethinking the traumatic experiences of childhood. Therefore, under the new rules, adult children of people who have socially unacceptable approaches to parenting should be exempt from the charge regardless of their financial situation. The Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club - Civic Platform, Modern, Polish Initiative, Greens (Dawn) will vote in favour of the proposed amendments to the Social Assistance Act. However, we have doubts about the question of verifying the applications submitted, Minister. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u233	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Health Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Public Health Act and some other laws in print no. 1631. At its 40th meeting on 29 October, the Sejm sent the above draft to the Health Committee for consideration. The Health Commission yesterday examined the above draft law on amending the Public Health Act and some other laws. The aim of this project is to set up a National Anti-Addictment Centre to ensure that public health activities are concentrated towards prevention and resolution of addiction problems in a single organisational unit as a national budgetary unit under the responsibility of the Health Minister. The Centre will be created on the basis of the combined two institutions, which at the moment carried out similar tasks, but within different ranges, i.e. The National Office for Drugs Prevention and the National Agency for Alcohol Troubleshooting. The Commission adopted 10 amendments in the course of its work, including: on the addition to the Public Health Council of a representative of the National Physiotherapist Board. So far, representatives of the other medical professions have worked in the Public Health Council, but there has been a shortage of physiotherapists. In this law we also introduce the possibility of cooperation of physiotherapists in the Council for Public Health. The new tax relief rules have also been brought into line with those of the Young Income Tax Act. In the course of the work, the amendment to supplement the provisions of part of the joint project with specialisation in the field of psychotherapy of addictions was also taken into account. At the moment, these provisions will be harmonised and more precise. Other amendments were made during the work, which were rejected by the committee, but were submitted as minority requests. These amendments concerned, among others, changes in the membership of the Anti-Addictment Board. The bill provides for the creation of such a council. In the bill we have entered institutions that can delegate to work in the Council on anti-drugs. On the other hand, some Members have tabled an amendment aimed at removing representatives from this project from the various ministries, including representatives from different organisations. These proposals have not been accepted and will be presented as minority proposals. An amendment was also presented to enable the Vice-President to invite addictive specialists and to appoint working groups. This competence has been assigned to the Chairman of the Council. I would like to point out that the Chairman of the Board is a member of the Ministry of Health's management and that the Vice-President is a member of the management of another Ministry. We have therefore considered that this amendment is not of major importance because both representatives represent the government, so there should not be any differences here and this provision, which is contained in the draft law, is also sufficient. Automatism was also proposed in obtaining a diploma of a specialist in psychotherapy addictions by people with certificates of a specialist in psychotherapy addictions. This amendment did not find recognition of the committee because such a provision would be contrary to the provisions of the Law on Specializations, which can be implemented in health care. There was precisely a normalized way, or path, to gain specialization in areas of health care. In view of the above, the committee, after considering the draft law presented at its meeting on 15 November, asks the Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. In accordance with the rules of procedure, the committee shall submit 10 minority applications at the request of the applicants. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u235	Dear Marshal, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position towards the government's draft law amending the Public Health Act and certain other laws, print Nos 1631 and 1741. The proposed law amends mainly the Public Health Act of 11 September 2015. The proposed amendments aim to set up a National Anti-Addictment Centre through the merger of the National Office for Drug Prevention and the National Agency for the Solution of Alcohol Problems. The proposed changes aim to focus public health tasks on prevention and resolution of problems related to different types of addictions in a single organisational unit as a national budgetary unit under the authority of the Minister responsible for Health. Focusing on the single decision-making entity that will be the centre will bring together issues of various addictions - functional, chemical, substance-related, illegal substances - at different stages of action to prevent addictions. Currently operating entities operate separately with regard to alcohol problems and the prevention of drug addiction without other addictions which remain outside the rules of the general law. Currently, each of these entities performs its statutory tasks and tasks of the National Health Programme related to separate types of addictions, although in many cases their combination would be justified and beneficial, as both entities carry out tasks of similar scope. The tasks of these actors relate jointly to prevention, treatment of addictions, public education and support for local self-government units in carrying out their statutory tasks concerning the prevention and resolution of alcohol problems as well as the prevention of drug addiction. A factor that differentiates the scope of the tasks of both actors is not the problem areas or forms of social impact, but merely the division into substances whose use causes damage and social problems. A centre dealing in a comprehensive way with addiction issues will enable state prevention to address addictions in a coordinated, coherent manner, which will contribute to the achievement of the planned objectives more effectively than ever before. The proposed changes will increase efficiency, reduce administrative burden, improve the current organisational solution and make better use of State budget resources and, above all, improve the efficiency of the activities and tasks carried out. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the draft law and recommends continuing legislative work on this project. Thank you very much. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in the second reading would like to make two amendments that will improve this project. Thank you very much. [[It's not a project.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u272	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them and certain other laws, prints No. 1731 and 1737. The Marshal of the Sejm, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure, sent the above proposal to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping for first reading on 8 November. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation carried out its first reading of the draft law on 15 November 2021. In the first reading, Members tabled three amendments to improve the functioning of the Act. The Legislative Office has tabled an amendment of a more precise nature to the law. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The proposed law is one of the elements of the so-called law. an anti-crisis shield introducing a number of regulatory solutions to minimise adverse effects on the economy and society as a result of the epidemic caused by COVID-19. The draft law amends the Law of 2 March 2020 on specific solutions related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them. U. poz. 1842, late. zm., hereinafter referred to as the Anti-COVID-19 Act, by the addition of an article. 15zzt, which will allow the validity of naval documents A diploma, a certificate of training, a certificate of the operator of radio equipment in the maritime service until the expiry of 90 days from the date of the cancellation of the epidemic or epidemic condition, whichever is the later. The proposed amendment to the validity of maritime documents is due to the deteriorating situation of seafarers who, due to restrictions introduced to prevent, counteract and combat COVID-19 in the Republic of Poland and other countries, have difficulties returning to the country and cannot renew their diploma, certificates, training or certificates of the operator of radio equipment in the maritime service or are unable to take appropriate training or to pass the examination necessary for the renewal of documents due to the restrictions introduced. In accordance with the International Convention on Standards for Training, Certification and Watchkeeping of Seafarers, they must be renewed during 5-year periods by completing updating training or passing an examination. Such training shall take place in maritime education units recognised by the Minister responsible for Maritime Affairs, the MJE, and shall consist of a theoretical and practical part. The lack of the possibility of stationary training prevents MJE training and thus the renewal of certificates by seafarers. The absence of such certificates shall prevent seafarers from embarking on a ship and shall terminate the contract for failure by the seafarer to hold appropriate qualification documents. In view of the above, it is proposed to add in the Against COVID-19 articles. 15zzt1 according to which where the term of validity of the diploma and the certificate of training referred to in Article 1 as appropriate. 63 points 1 and 3 of the Maritime Safety Act of 18 August 2011 or the certificate of the operator of radio equipment in the maritime service referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 1a of the Law of 16 July 2004 on telecommunications law expires between 1 February 2020 and 13 March 2020, the term of validity of those documents will be extended until 90 days from the date of the cancellation of the epidemic or epidemic threat, whichever is later. The recommended solution shall take into account the guidelines set out in International Maritime Organisation circular letter No 4204 of 2 April 2020 concerning guidelines for the certification of seafarers and fishermen. According to Art. 15zzzzl lips. 2 The Antitrust Act COVID-19 grants from the State budget to finance the Agency's implementation of the tasks referred to in Article. 15zzzzl lips. 1 of this bill. An entity obliged to pay a grant pursuant to Article. 15zzzzl lips. 3 of the Antitrust Act COVID-19 is the Minister responsible for Transport. The draft law amends the Law of 4 September 1997 on the departments of government administration, Dz. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1893, which aim to optimise the functioning of administrative structures. The project proposes changes in matters assigned to the water management department. It is assumed that the Minister responsible for water management will take over the melioration tasks from the Minister responsible for rural development. This amendment aims to ensure that a coherent system of water melioration is set up by the water management authority. Water melioration consists of regulating water relations in order to improve soil production capacity and facilitate soil cultivation. This action is one way of using water resources. The measures taken in the area of drainage by the Minister responsible for water management should therefore be carried out in close cooperation with the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development, which is competent in matters relating to agricultural chambers, farmers' trade unions and socio-occupational organisations of farmers, i.e. representatives directly interested in improving water conditions for agriculture. The epidemic threat associated with the COVID-19 pandemic has also affected schools and educational institutions. It is therefore proposed to add provisions amending the Law of 31 March 2020 amending the Law on specific solutions related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19 and other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them and certain other laws. The amendment consists in the addition to Article. 65 ww. Paragraph 5 is replaced by the following: The amount of assigned revenue in accordance with Article 21 (3) of the Financial Regulation is estimated at EUR 100000000. Amendments to the Act of 15 July 2020 on the Tourist Bone, OJ OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 839, in particular, they aim to postpone the deadline for making payments by means of a tourist voucher. Amendment to Article Paragraph 7 1 and 2 of the Act of 15 July 2020 on the Polish Tourist Bone extending the deadline of 30 March 2022 to 30 September 2022 for payment for hotel services or tourist events by means of a Polish Tourist Bonus will create a vast majority of persons entitled to use this voucher for its full use. The draft law does not require presentation to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union, including the European Central Bank. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Commission adopted the draft law without objection. I recommend the High House to adopt a proposed law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u310	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the parliamentary club, the Left has the honour to present to the High House our position towards the government draft law amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws. Poland has been facing a difficult demographic situation for years, and this problem can grow in the years to come. We can deal in the long term with the burden on the pension system, the health care system or the destabilisation of the democratic system. At present, the lack of an adequate number of workers in the labour market makes our economy need support from foreigners. We believe that the Polish immigration policy should be based on the quota system and provide for an easy way of acquiring a title for permanent residence and legal employment. Today, almost 800,000 people work in Poland. people from abroad. It's over 11%. 60% more than at the end of 2019 employed foreigners are employees employed for employment contracts. 3% of them are engaged in business activities and others benefit from a different form of employment. Nearly 3/4 of foreigners employed in Poland are Ukrainians. They fill gaps in the labour market, contribute to GDP growth and social security contributions. Unfortunately, it seems that Poland copies the German, French model from the 1950s and 1960s, when Western countries used the work of foreigners without settling them permanently, i.e. they used the so-called. Gastarbeiter model. We can pay for this in the future. Currently, the visa issue process is not guaranteed continuity. This sometimes results in downtimes in the good operation of procedures so far, which can translate into problems with issuing visas for Ukrainian citizens and the supply of people from the East to the Polish labour market. The Ombudsman of small and medium-sized enterprises has been actively requesting action since the beginning of 2019 to remedy the chronicity of proceedings under consideration by the Voivodship Offices in matters relating to the employment of foreigners. The government, recognizing the problems of entrepreneurs and at the same time trying to support them in supplementing the staff shortages of workers from the East, submitted a change to the Act on Foreigners. This law represents a revolution in facilitating the employment of foreigners on simplified terms. Currently, Polish companies can draw employees from the former Soviet Union, Armenia, Belarus, Georgia, Moldova, Russia and Ukraine on simplified rules, but only for a period of 6 months. This period will be extended to 2 years. After the changes to the law to bring such an employee to Poland, it is enough for the entrepreneur to issue a declaration about entrusting the work to a foreigner. The basic change is to extend the working life of the foreigner from 6 months to 24 months. It's supposed to relieve the work of the voivodship offices. The foreigner will be able to work for up to 2 years on the basis of the so-called. a statement and obtaining a work permit for him will no longer be necessary. The simplified procedure for granting temporary residence and work permits will apply to proceedings initiated before 1 January 2022. The procedures for granting work permits to foreigners carried out by the voivodship offices will be accelerated. The requirement for the foreigner to present the address of residence in Poland as necessary for the issue of a residence and work permit in our country will be waived. The amendment also contains a provision that a foreigner will have to receive at least a minimum wage regardless of the employment he is employed for. The Act abolishes the mode of modification of the temporary residence permit and work in the event of a change of the entity entrusting the performance of the work. This authorisation shall not specify the entity entrusting the performance of the work or the conditions under which it is carried out. These will be determined after the decision to grant the permit has been delivered by the foreigner who will submit to the voivodship a statement of the entrepreneur entrusting him with his work. The catalogue of circumstances that do not require a change of the temporary residence and work permit shall be extended. Entities of special strategic importance for our economy will obtain a permit for work and temporary residence of a foreigner more quickly. Disappearing has a 12-month period, which will allow the employee to be extended continuously on the basis of further declarations. This will result in the disappearance of D05a visas and there will be no need to limit their validity to 6 months. According to the amendment, the obligation of health insurance will be considered to be fulfilled when it comes to the employment of a foreigner in Poland. The Act also makes it easier to apply for the Polish Charter. Entrepreneurs hope that the Ministry of the Family will benefit from changes in the possibility of extending the list of countries from which these workers can be recruited, or will define rules that will allow us to use a simplified procedure when bringing specialists to Poland. My club supports the solutions adopted, with one amendment being tabled. We also wanted to draw attention, Minister, to the fact that very many visas are not issued, among others. for Indians or Nepaleses. We ask for some solutions for these people, because it is tens of thousands of people who are waiting to find a job in Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Because we know that very often at the moment because of the fact that processing as well as mediation in Poland is primarily in the hands of large private companies, mainly foreign companies... Because if they were Polish companies, I think it would be much easier, but they are usually foreign companies and companies that use their position and position very strongly, because they are strong companies in this whole food chain, they use the way that farmers pay very low prices primarily for soft fruit, but not only for soft fruit, but also for all agricultural products. It happens that a farmer brings his products to the market, and in fact even if he unloads his products, he doesn't know at what price the companies will pay. Therefore, there is a need for such a company in Poland, a state-owned company. It would be good if it was a state-owned company that would influence prices, and it would have an impact on buying. And that's what the holding company is for. The holding is established in the Ministry of State Assets and on the basis of a company of the Treasury, a sugar company. In order to create this holding, it is also necessary to transfer other companies which are now in the hands or in the management of the National Agricultural Support Centre, which is under the Minister of Agriculture. It is necessary to transfer these companies to the Ministry of State Assets, to transfer them just to create this holding. There was some doubt, legal doubt, whether, on the basis of this right which we have at this moment, it is possible to transfer the assets of these companies to the Ministry of State Assets, to another department, for the management of another department. In order to resolve this question, this draft law, a parliamentary bill, is introduced, which actually has only two articles, which in fact legally develops these doubts, that KOWR, the National Agricultural Support Centre, will be able to transfer the assets of those companies that are needed to create this holding company legally. That is why this bill is presented to the High House for resolution. As far as what was going on in committee, in the context of the committee's work report, there were, above all, no votes against here. There were many questions from the opposition, but these were really strict substantive questions which, I think, show that everyone takes this issue very seriously, to the formation of this holding company. The result of the vote was, of course, a large majority of 30 votes, 30 parliamentarians were behind this bill, but 8 Members abstained. Here we regret this fact, because on the one hand the Members of the opposition say that the holding company is very much needed, that this holding company should already be, why it is not yet there, and when it comes to what, when we go to such a specific one: I check, voting, either there are Members against or they are holding back. In this case, it was abstentions. That raises a lot of doubts. Although we are talking to Members who know that this holding is very much needed and that this holding company will help the Polish farmer, we see in the vote that it is different. What are we really regretting and regretting Polish farmers? The fact that something else is said, and the other thing is in action. To make that happen, that's the need. just such a company, such a holding company, which will help the Polish farmer. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u374	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets and the Committee on Local Government and Regional Policy on the Government's draft law on compensation for income lost by the municipalities in 2018 due to the change in the scope of taxation of wind power plants, contained in print No 1676. The above project entered the marshal's staff on 22 October 2021 and was directed by the Marshal of the Sejm on 26 October this year. to the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy for first reading. On 15 November, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] laying down detailed rules for the application of Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial Regulation applicable to the I would like to draw attention to the fact that the regulations proposed in the proposal aim at the implementation of the Constitutional Tribunal ruling of 22 July 2020, sygn. Act K 4/19. This judgment concerned the examination of the constitutionality of the Article. 17 point 2 of the Act of 7 June 2018 amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act and certain other laws to the extent that it has caused the effects of retro-treatment, i.e. the characteristics of retroactive legal standards, in particular as regards the revision of the definition of structures and the definition of wind power plants. In its judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal ruled that the Article was unconstitutional. 17 point 2 of the Renewable Energy Act to the extent that it infringes the principle of non-retroactivity, and obliged the legislator to undertake legislative work to develop a regulation to compensate the municipalities for the losses incurred as a result of the implementation of the Renewable Energy Sources Act. As a result of the analysis of the above mentioned above, the judgment of the Council of Ministers has decided to draw up a draft law dedicated to the units of the local government of the first level, as there are currently no provisions on the basis of which compensation payments could be made to the municipalities designated by the Court. Therefore, the implementation of the judgment of the Court can only be implemented by adopting the relevant draft resolution. The proposed deadline for payment of PLN 554 million is 2022. There were a few questions during the discussion, but no one has tabled any amendments to the proposed proposal. I ask the High House kindly to support this project. Thank you. [[Applause]] Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Tomasz Ławniczak, Law and Justice will be the first to speak. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I just wanted to add to that, because I will no longer duplicate what I said here, that it is not, as some think, a windmill law that introduced the 10H rule, but it is a revision of this law from June 2018, which introduced changes in the Construction Act - this is a definition of structures - and the law on wind power plants in terms of the definition of wind power plants. That's what caused the tax changes. The municipalities, when planning the 2018 budget, provided that they would receive income from real estate tax by charging real estate tax on wind power plants, and during the year, the Sejm made changes to the fact that power plants are not subject to this tax. The municipalities therefore appealed to the Constitutional Court. The Constitutional Tribunal delivered its judgment in June 2020. He's given it 18 months. This is simply the execution of this judgment. After a few speeches at the joint high committees, which concerned provisions proposed by the government bill, we came - at least that's what I felt - to the consensus. The discussion will show how the procedure will take place over the above law. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

The distance law, as it was called, increased the taxation of wind power plants in 2016 with a property tax constituting the sole income of the municipalities. Previously, this tax was paid only on the construction part and accounted for 30% of the turbine value. Since 2017, the property tax has also been imposed on the technical part. This solution was abolished retroactively in 2018 - since the beginning of 2018. - as incompatible with the international obligations of Poland. However, the municipalities have already taken account of the increased revenue in their budgets and have therefore planned a correspondingly higher expenditure. Therefore, they were deprived of the expected revenue because they had to repay the unduly collected tax for 2018. This, in turn, caused that they could not make many investments previously planned and could not benefit, for example. from various projects carried out from the European Union. For example, Darłowo village lost about PLN 15 million for this reason. As a result of the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal which ruled that the Article was unconstitutional. 17 point 2 of the RES Act, the legislator must today make compensation for local governments. This compensation concerns about 200 municipalities in which wind power plants are located. This compensation is intended to amount to PLN 524 million. These appropriations will be paid to local authorities after appropriate applications have been submitted to the voivodeship under the special reserve of the State budget in 2022. High Izbo! After reading this bill, knowing that nowadays every penny and every zloty for the local government is important, we will support this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u424	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Minister! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the Parliamentary draft law on one-off provision of money to anti-communist activists and politically repressed persons, print No. 1730, and the printing of the report is printing No. 1747. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 8 November 2021, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for first reading. At yesterday's meeting, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy carried out the first reading and examination of the above draft law. The bill is the next step, it is a valuable initiative towards legal, financial and moral redress for people who fought for our freedom. Procededed project, i.e. The law on the one-off provision of money to activists of the anti-communist opposition and to those repressed for political reasons shall lay down the conditions for the acquisition of the right to a single benefit and the rules for the payment of that benefit. The purpose of a single benefit is to provide additional income support to persons with a confirmed status as an anti-communist opposition activist or a person repressed for political reasons. The right to this benefit will be exercised by persons who have obtained the status of an anti-communist opposition activist and a politically repressed person on the basis of a decision of the Head of the Office for Combatants and Persons Repressed until 31 December 2021. A person with a confirmed status of anti-communist opposition activist and a politically repressed person will be entitled to a single payment. The benefit will be paid out of office by the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons in the amount of PLN 3000 and will be granted through an administrative decision. The decision on a single payment will be issued by the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons. A decision granting a single cash benefit shall not be justified and shall be subject to immediate enforceability. The contested law proposes that the benefit should be free of execution and not be included in the income entitlement to benefits and allowances granted on the basis of separate provisions, and that it should be free of personal income tax. The project is another bill in the six-year process of restoring the dignity of anti-communist opposition activists. I would like to remind you that the Law and Justice Government has repeatedly amended the Law of 20 March 2015 on anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons, and its changes were moving towards an extension of the support given above. group. It is worth mentioning that since 15 October 2020, a compensatory allowance has been granted if a person with a confirmed status of an anti-communist opposition activist or a politically repressed person receives a pension or invalidity pension or a pension for incapacity for work of less than 2 501,76 gross zloty per month. In these circumstances, the person shall be entitled to the compensation of the benefit collected up to that amount. Also in this year. new regulations have entered into force to increase the availability of monetary assistance. They included an increase in the amount and frequency of one-off monetary aid granted, an increase in the income criteria by 50% as regards one-off aid and interim aid. A new way of establishing the basis for the assessment of initial capital has also been introduced, recognising as periods of premium periods of political repression while accepting the amount of average remuneration as the basis for the assessment of contributions during that period. This means that the pensions of these people will be higher. Important workers' rights have also been introduced: anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons remaining in employment have an additional 5-day leave per calendar year. These are only the most important benefits that have been introduced by the Law of 2015 by the Government of Law and Justice. It's a way of repairing the harm that many people have suffered. To compare support - and mostly to you and to those who listen to us, numbers - I will say yes. As regards the funds that were allocated to combatants and anti-communist opposition activists in 2015, the figure was 9 million. At the moment, this figure is 130 million. This number is really impressive. That's a lot of support for these people. All actions lead to making amends to those who, at the time, during the martial law, have suffered enormous moral, financial losses since 1956. In view of the above, in accordance with the findings of the committee which unanimously requested this, I propose that the High Sejm adopt a draft law to be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

There are 15 218 people who have received the status of an anti-communist opposition activist or a politically repressed person, 13 731 of whom live. After death, because it is acceptable, this is the status I granted to 286 people at the request of families. After confirming the status during these 6 years of operation of the Act, 1199 people died. Ladies and gentlemen! As the parliamentarians have said here, there has been a major change in the care of anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons. The Act, which the High Sejm adopted in 2015, has emerged from this group deserved in the fight for freedom of Poland another group of subjects of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons. But in the wake of this, apart from the honorable title, legitimacy, badge and certain forms of financial support, but for those who were in a really tragic material situation, because the original wording of the bill introduced income thresholds were draconian... The point is that, in fact, its status was purely honorable and only three amendments: from 2017, 2020 and 2021, have made this group a group of several thousand who have the same powers as combatants of the war period, i.e. those who are subject to the Combatant Act who fought arms in hand. And in some cases these powers are even slightly higher. And just to sort it out, so we also know that it's not like this bill is a one-off sum of 3,000. zł and the Polish state do not support those who fought for freedom. Well, every anti-communist opposition activist has the right to provide money for life, every month, currently in the amount of PLN 460. That's the same amount as the combatants get in the war period. Activists finally have the opportunity to benefit, already twice a year, from one-off or periodic assistance, for example. they must adapt the housing to the needs of persons with disabilities. They also have the right to benefit outside the order of health care and pharmaceutical services provided in pharmacies. They have the right to use outpatient specialized services without referring to a health care professional. They have priority when it comes to accessing environmental social protection. Double consideration shall be given to the duration of their activities, internment or repression in establishing initial capital. As a result of the last year's amendment, they also have 50% tariff breaks for public transport and a 51 percent reduction for public transport by rail. They also have the right to use health insurance, which the head of office pays directly if necessary. We have also created the possibility for activists to perform care tasks. And it worked extremely well during the pandemic, when also opposition activists, apart from combatants, were involved in aiding, for example, the provision of meals, lunches home to minimize their contact with the outside world. Finally, a very important amendment from last year. The opposition activists have the right to compensation. If they have a pension lower than currently PLN 2501 and there are many such persons in this group of 13 thousand, they have the right to a compensatory allowance which is paid by pension authorities. Importantly, also thanks to last year's amendment, monetary aid granted by the head of office is exempted from bail-out. We know that they are often people who are in a difficult situation in this regard. Finally, this year activists were given the right to benefit from a new way of establishing core capital for periods considered as contributions. They shall have the right to additional 5-day leave in the calendar year and shall be covered by the programmes which the office carries out. For example, we finance anti-communist opposition activists staying in spas and sanatoriums. They're not waiting two, three years to go to the sanatorium. They choose a sanatorium, and the office, on the basis of an invoice issued by the spa, in the amount specified in the regulations, pays them for their stay and health care. Similarly, they are covered by a programme to finance the purchase of hearing aids, under which the Authority finances such apparatus up to 10 000. zł on both ears, and this program enjoys, as in the case of combatants, great interest. In answer to the questions of ladies and gentlemen of Members and in ordering certain matters, with regard to the question of Mr Jan Szopiński, I would like to say that the status of anti-communist opposition activist is confirmed by the head of the Veterans' Office at the request of the person who wishes to have such status. Before such status is confirmed, administrative proceedings shall be carried out. First of all, it begins with the process of self-illustration, i.e. the person applying for the status of an activist, must submit the decision of the President of the Institute of National Remembrance that she did not cooperate with the security authorities of the communist state. Usually, in response to further questions, this status validation process, if the case is very simple and confirmed by evidence, documents, witness statements, takes 30 days. If the case is a little more complicated, because there is no evidence at the Institute of National Remembrance, no testimony of witnesses, according to the Act such cases are dealt with by the Regional Council for Anti-Communist Opposition Actors. Their recommendation to the office is not binding, but it is very helpful. As regards the measures that are granted for this purpose, anti-communist opposition activists - in relation to questions by Anna April and Jan Mosiński. Ladies and gentlemen, in 2015 for assistance and for combatants, and for anti-communist opposition activists, the office for combatants had a budgeted amount of PLN 9 million per year. This year it is the amount of PLN 130 million. And you will note that, especially in the veteran environment, unfortunately due to the inexorable laws of biology, the drain of our subjects is significant and the average age of war veterans is now 94 years. These are the two values: 9 million in 2015 and 130 million this year. Mrs Zofia Czernow asked what percentage of activists are benefiting from and not benefiting. Ladies and gentlemen, the status is over 13,000. activists at their request. If we get a total of 30,000. people, this will be a very good result, given that after 6 years the status has, including dead persons, 15 thousand. persons. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u466	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on Infrastructure, I have the honour to present a report on the joint meeting of the High Commissions. On the basis of the article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm at its 42nd meeting on 16 November 2021, following its first reading, he addressed a draft law amending the Act on local taxes and charges, print No 1729, and the Senate draft law on local taxes and charges, print No 919 to a joint review by the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. At its joint meeting, the Commission considered it a leading government project to amend the Local Tax and Charges Act, print No 1729, and examined it in detail. One of the most important changes to the contested law is to clarify the scope of the exemption of land with rail infrastructure from property tax occupied by entities which do not carry out railway activities. Currently, regulations in the article. Paragraph 7 1 The Act of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges exempts land on which the railway infrastructure is located from real estate tax. This provision is used to avoid taxing the land on which the railway infrastructure is located by non-rail operators. The interpretation of the rules allowed to avoid tax on property tax, among others. through mines or shopping galleries, resulting in the loss of part of the income by the municipalities. The contested law proposes amendments to the article. 7, which seal the exemption scheme and exclude the possibility of using a real estate tax exemption in the case of land or parts of land occupied by undertakings other than those relating to the performance of the railway infrastructure manager's tasks or the provision of services by the operator of a service facility within the meaning of Annex 2 paragraph. 2 and 3 to the Rail Transport Act. Two amendments were tabled during the committee meeting. The first of an editorial and technical nature, the second of which introduces significant substantive changes. The second amendment amended the date of entry into force of the Act of 1 January 2023 as at 1 January 2022. The use of the tax exemption by unauthorised entities motivates the implementation of the rules as soon as possible. The amendment also introduced a provision on the tax exemption for land property, buildings and buildings that are part of a service facility, referring to freight terminals. In view of the issue of notification, the date of entry into force of the regulation in this field remained within 1 January 2023. The amendment received the support of the committee and unanimously over political divisions was adopted by 36 votes in favour. All amendments tabled and the entire bill received a positive recommendation from high committees. The Commissions, after considering the draft law, request the adoption by the High House of the law together with the amendments tabled. Ladies and gentlemen! I also thank you very much on my behalf for being able to change, in particular, the entry into force of the entry into force of 1 January 2023 to 1 January 2022. This is a matter that has touched the local government very much. I am very pleased that we have been able to carry out this change together. Thank you very much.

As a result of this gap, the tax exemption is applied to different activities, provided that it is carried out on railway land. This is, of course, unacceptable, because this exemption should be granted only to those who carry out railway activities on these railway land, i.e. as regards railway infrastructure or rail transport. And this bill is normal for this situation. I think that the discussion within the committee was very cultural and, most importantly, effective. As a result of this discussion, the original date of entry into force of the law, which was proposed by the government on 1 January 2023, changed. As a result of the amendments which have been notified, the Act will apply, with a small exception, from 1 January 2022. I would like to thank you for adopting this amendment. Two amendments were tabled on this subject. One of them was the amendment of the Parliamentary Circle of Poland 2050, which I had the pleasure of personally reporting during the committee meeting. This project is intended to avoid a dispute with the European Commission, which called on Poland to address its failure to provide adequate safeguards for the conservation of forest habitats and plant and animal species in accordance with the requirements of Council Directive 92/43/EEC of 21 May 1992 on the conservation of natural habitats and of wild fauna and flora, i.e. the Habitats Directive, and Directive 2009/147/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 November 2009 on the conservation of wild birds, so-called. Birds Directives. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No and Article. 9 Birds Directives. High Izbo! Of course, the government does not want this dispute with the European Commission to escalate, and this is where the bill we are talking about is prepared. The amendments contained in this Act include: firstly, repealing the Forest Law, which concerns the requirements of good forest management practice and amending the Nature Conservation Act concerning these requirements of good practice; secondly, adding an article in the Nature Conservation Act. 52b obliging forest owners to apply the requirements of good forest management practice, both in the preparation phase and in the implementation phase of forest management activities, and a provision containing a statutory delegation for the Minister responsible for Forest Management. the environment to define, by means of a regulation, the requirements of good forest management practice taking into account the protection of species, objectives, forest management principles and economic, social and cultural requirements - the same code of good practice will prevent potential collisions in forest management activities with environmental elements; thirdly, clarification in this draft Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 5 Nature Conservation Act based on Art. 12 and Article 12 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 13 of the Habitats Directive by indicating that derogations do not apply to species subject to strict protection under the Directives. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo!
