High Izbo! During the work carried out after the first reading, the committee examined the request for a public hearing of the bill. The proposal provided for a public hearing on 6 December, which would obviously lead to the failure of the designated environmental committee to comply with the deadline for the submission of a report on the project, which is already mentioned on 15 November. This proposal was not supported by a majority of committee members. During the detailed work on the draft, the committee rejected in the vote three packages of amendments tabled by Members of the opposition clubs. These amendments included the deletion of Article. 1 of the project, which would lead to the leaving of Article in the Forest Act. 14b, and that's the article. 14b is most criticised by the European Commission. This is an article which, according to the European Commission, constitutes a broad derogation from prohibitions on highly protected species, in particular species subject to strict protection under the Birds and Habitats Directives. The objective of the amendment, namely the elimination of the dispute with the European Commission, would therefore not be achieved. Of course, these amendments were rejected. In fact, it was that the packages of amendments that were submitted by different clubs were simply repeating themselves. It is difficult to talk about this in detail at the moment because of the lack of time. It should be stressed that the obligations of the owner of the forest, which have so far been laid down in Article. 14b - are defined because this article is still there - they are written in the Nature Conservation Act in the added article. 52b in accordance with the arrangements made by the Government with the European Commission. The second of the problems identified in the amendments under consideration is the restoration of an appeal to Annex II of the Habitats Directive in the Nature Conservation Act. Let me remind you that the Habitats Directive identifies plant and animal species and habitat types important for the Community, for which a Natura 2000 network has been established. Annex II to the Habitats Directive sets out the plant and animal species important for the Community, the protection of which requires the designation of special protection areas, and Annex IV sets out the plant and animal species important for the Community which require strict protection. Therefore, reference to the Annex II, which is present in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of This is not part of the directive. The European Commission considers this unnecessary, unnecessary and, as I say, goes beyond the requirements of the Directive. For this reason, this was removed from the project under discussion from print no. 1728. That is why it was removed from this editorial unit in the government submission. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u502	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club - Civic Platform, Modern, Polish Initiative, Greens I have the honour to present a position towards the government's draft law on amending the Law on Forests and the Act on Nature Protection from Print No. 1728. Ladies and gentlemen, this law is a powerful example of the fact that neither Poles nor Poles nor Polish nature can afford the rule of law and justice. I'll explain why. The project we are discussing is aimed at partially really violating EU law in response to the so-called. the infringement procedure initiated by the European Commission against Poland in 2018 and subsequently due to the failure of Poland to respond properly to the Commission's complaint to the TSUE lodged on 15 July this year. According to the content of the complaint, the first part of the complaint, Poland violated the obligations arising from the provisions of the Council Directive called the Habitats Directive and another directive called the Birds Directive. How? It simply created a broad circumvention of European law by means of a regulation and thus almost completely excluded the management of the Polish forest, including the protection of valuable, dying species of fauna and flora, from EU law. In fact, the Commission notes that Poland has created legal fiction in terms of compliance with conservation orders. In the second equally important part of the complaint, the European Commission argues that Poland illegally restricts its citizens' access to environmental information and the right to an effective judicial procedure. It therefore infringes again the Habitats Directive and the Birds Directive, but also the Treaty on European Union, the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union and the Aarhus Convention. The bill we are discussing today is an answer to the first part of the complaint, and this is unfortunately an incomplete and questionable answer. Here are some of them. Removing the article. 14b. 3 of the Forest Act is a positive change but already delete paragraph. 1 and 2 removes a number of obligations imposed by the law on forest owners to protect them. In turn, the proposed Article. 52b in the Nature Conservation Act to which the institution of good forest practice has been transferred raises questions of interpretation. Delete Annex II from the proposed Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. The 5th Nature Conservation Act will constitute a further infringement of the Habitats Directive. There's such a real threat. Criticism is also due to the Ministry of Climate and Environment for the way in which the legislative process is being conducted, because once again the public's participation has been overlooked, and yet, of the horror, just restricting access, the public to environmental information concerns the second part of the European Commission's complaint, which has triggered the need for this revision. These are just some of the reservations I would like to draw your attention to. But there is an even bigger problem. The government amendment we are discussing today does not refer at all to this second, very important Commission complaint, part of the European Commission's complaint against Poland. The Commission raises the problem of Poland's infringement of both natural directives and a number of basic routes. The Polish government disagrees with this opinion and will not present us, as the Minister announced at the committee meeting, even a draft solution to this problem, because it disagrees with the Commission's complaint and the case is in the Court. What does that mean? This means risking further penalties for failure to comply with the law. Already today, Polish taxpayers pay for non-compliance by the Government of Law and Justice, i.e. One million euros a day for the functioning of the Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court and 500,000. Euro per day for each day of operation of the mine in Turów, that is, please, over 7 million Polish zlotys per day. These are penalties that are already imposed on us today for non-compliance with the law and for creating a legal bypass, such as those contained by the current government in the current version of the law that we are updating. I warn you, as we warned as the Green Party, in Turowa's case this project does not constitute a full response to the complaint to the TSUE and this project puts us under further punishment. As the Civic Coalition Club, I therefore make amendments to this project and I call on the Ministry and the Government to correct errors from the second part of the European Commission's complaint. Without these corrective measures, we will be against this project, because this is, Minister, a ticking environmental and financial bomb, especially since we are dealing with a deep climate crisis and the extinction of species, we really need to protect them. Polish taxpayers and the Polish environment cannot afford the rule of law and justice today. [[Applause]]

The evaluation of the implementation of these directives, as already stated in the rapporteur's report, resulted in indications and a call for the removal of deficiencies in 2018. 14b Law on Forests together with Code of Good Practice, which is assessed in the committee in a collision with parts of the article. 6, Art. 12 and Article 12 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 16 the Habitats Directive and Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) a, b and d and Article 4 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. 9 Birds The amendment of these laws is therefore a response to the shortcomings identified by the European Commission regarding these laws. It consists, first, of removing the provisions of good practice from the Forest Act, second, of introducing amended provisions on good forest management practice into the Nature Conservation Act. The Commission for Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies, together with the Commission for Public Finance, today considered an urgent government bill on specific rules for the remuneration of persons performing cybersecurity tasks, print No 1743. The project envisages the creation of a Cybersecurity Fund to finance ICT benefits for people working in cybersecurity jobs in the country. This will ensure competitive remuneration for cybersecurity professionals employed in public administration and special services. The creation of the Cybersecurity Fund is part of the objectives set out in the Cybersecurity Strategy of the Republic of Poland for the years 2019 The Fund will serve the implementation of specific and specific tasks. The amount of remuneration for work or salary, including the provision of ICT, may be up to a maximum of 21 times the basic amount for members of the civil service corps as laid down in the Budget Act. The draft rules lay down the basic principles relating to the granting, payment and withdrawal of the ICT benefit. The proposed solution fosters the provision of highly qualified staff to public entities dealing with state cybersecurity. It will allow for halting the drain of skilled workers and the employment of new experts. At today's joint committee meeting, five substantive amendments were tabled by members of the Law and Justice Club and a package of legislative amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau. They were adopted and the whole of the law was positively assessed by the joint committees. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u542	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! This is a law that should not cause much controversy, because we are all aware of the need for it. It is a law that says that all people working for Poland and who are protecting our security in cyberspace will be able to receive financial allowances. The purpose of this regulation is to ensure that these salaries in the various institutions are at a similar level so that they can compete with the market, which has now greatly accelerated the increase in the remuneration of cybersecurity experts and that those experts we have, whom Poland has developed, trained and who are excellent professionals, maintain and resist competition from the private market. The project envisages the creation of a Cybersecurity Fund, which will finance ICT benefits for people working in cybersecurity jobs in a country in key areas of state security. Its responsibility will be the Minister responsible for IT. The creation of the Cybersecurity Fund is part of the objectives set out in the Cybersecurity Strategy of the Republic of Poland for the years 2019 The provisions of the draft law lay down the conditions for applying for funds from the Cybersecurity Fund to cover the costs of ICT provision and other costs associated with it. The draft rules lay down the basic principles relating to the granting, payment and withdrawal of the ICT benefit. Thus, the proposed solution promotes the provision of highly qualified staff to public entities engaged in cybersecurity. This will stop the drain of skilled workers and the employment of new experts. The strengthening of staff in the area of cybersecurity is particularly important due to the significant increase in incidents and cybercrime at present. ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u696	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As every year, celebrations related to the 103rd anniversary of Poland's regaining independence were celebrated in many towns on 11 November. This year in Tomaszów Lubelski these celebrations were unique, because the first Arch of Victory commemorating the 100th anniversary of the Battle of Warsaw and the 100th anniversary of Marshal Józef Piłsudski's stay in Tomaszów Lubelski was unveiled in Poland, when on March 20, 1921 in the Siwa Valley there were regiments fighting in the Battle of Komarów. As one of the initiators of the project and honorary chairman of the Tomaszewski Committee of celebrations of the 100th anniversary of the Battle of Warsaw, I am pleased that we managed to involve a very large group of people in the creation of the monument. In particular, I would like to thank the elders of Tomaszowski, Henryk Karwan, Jarosław Korzeni, Wójtek Dariusz Kozłowski, Piotr Pasieczny, Roman Miedziak, Jack Wiśniewski, Mayor Mark Łuszczynski, Ryszard Madziar, Mayor of Wołomin in 20102014, Robert Czysz, Museum Director, Andrzej Bulicz, Head of the Faculty of Education, Designers and Performers. The exposed Arch of Victory is located on the square of the 1st High School of General Education. Bartosz Głowacki is 6.5 m high and over 4 m wide and is decorated with bronze castings. The symbolism of the arc is very significant. On the eastern side dedicated to the Battle of Warsaw centrally there are figures of charging a horse, footman and Fr. Ignacy Shells. On the pillars of the arch is the image of Our Lady of Grace and the legion eagle. On the western side dedicated to Józef Piłsudski, a relief of the bust of the marshal was centrally placed, while on the pillars - the Virtuti Militari Cross and the sign of the foresters who supported Piłsudski during his activities in the conspiracy. The buildings of the Arch of Victory were accompanied by a number of patriotic projects. One of them was to commemorate places that were real symbols of regaining independence. Together with the initiators, we were in Oleandra, where Józef Piłsudski and his first staff brigade set out in 1914. We visited the places of battles: Radzymin, where some of the heaviest fights took place during the Battle of Warsaw, Ossów, the town where scouts, students and students first fought against the Bolsheviks, Komarów, where the Bolshevik horse army of Budionny was defeated. We also went to Lviv and Vilnius. From all these towns we received from people who took care of the land, which in special urns was placed at the base of the arch on the map of the reborn Second Republic. This exhibition is enriched with artifacts from the years 1918 to 1920 and symbols of defeated conquerors: German and Austrian are shattered replicas of coats of arms, Russian - broken into half Bolshevik sickle and hammer. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The unveiling of the Arch of Victory was a great patriotic manifestation, during which the inhabitants of the city and the county gathered many times. I invite everyone to Tomaszów Lubelski. I sincerely hope that thanks to this construction, incomprehensible inability will be broken and new monuments commemorating the Battle of Warsaw will be erected very quickly, including the most important arch of victory in Warsaw. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u700	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! One of the few. On the shadow of the next anniversary of regaining independence there is another date of a completely different nature. On November 12, 2004, Stanisław Skalski, the first Polish aviation ace of World War II, left for the eternal service, a man faithful to his beliefs. He was born in Kresy in 1915. After graduation he starts his studies at the University of Technology, but fascinated by aviation he abandons her and starts his studies at the Dęblin School of Aviation. He completes his higher piloting course and begins his service in the 142nd hunting squadron in Toruń. In 1938, he was promoted to lieutenant. He has the reputation of a horned soul and a uncompromising man who even superiors can respond in an uncensored way. He was disciplined three times before the war for insulting his superior, deliberately opening a parachute too late and publicly expressing his willingness to fight against the Japanese on China's side. His feud, which caused him so much trouble during the war, was a blessing. Within a few days of the September campaign, five German planes are shot down, becoming an unwritten ace of Polish aviation. He's doing an unprecedented job landing next to a German plane that's been shot down and helping his crew. During the battle for England, he fought in the 501 British fighter squadron, shooting down more German planes. He flew with success in Polish hunting divisions. He fought in Africa as part of an unofficial unit called the Skalski Circus, with a lot of victories. He's one of the most experienced Allied fighter pilots. He was awarded many high Polish and British decorations, including Virtuti Militari. The British offer him very prestigious leadership positions in the Royal Air Force, but Skalski refuses. After the end of the war, he decides without hesitation to return to Poland. However, it was not the Poland he knew and dreamed of. Nevertheless, as one of the few pilots returning from the West, he begins to work in the army as a piloting technician. When he engages in the search for a pilot Władysław Śliwiński arrested by security, he is arrested and sentenced to death after a very brutal investigation for alleged espionage for British intelligence. This is where Skalski's incompetence came to light again. All the way through the investigation, he didn't confess to anything and said he was innocent. He thought that if he had written a request for pardon, he would have confessed to the charges. He's been on death row for over a year, knowing that any keys or footsteps in the hallway can be a prelude to the execution of the death penalty. However, Skalski's close friends had no doubt, thinking that the most important thing is that he survives. The pilot's mother turned to Bolesław Bierut, who changed his sentence for life. After several years of legal battles on a wave of thaws in 1956. Stanisław Skalski is getting out of prison. Aviation is his great love, so he decides to join the army again and undergos training on jet planes. After 1989, he tries to get involved in a new political life, which is not particularly good for him. In his old age, in the last years of his life he was completely robbed, died alone in a social home. Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u708	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In Warsaw, the civic budget, which has been implemented since 2014, was initially called a participatory budget. This budget was intended to serve as an action whereby residents directly decide on the allocation of a small part of the budget of the local government unit. Each government has its own rules on the basis of which the citizen's budget operates. It usually appears that projects are submitted after the call for proposals to the civil budget. In Warsaw, they are projects on a regional and urban level. These projects are first assessed by officials in terms of compliance with city policy. And here we have the first place where it comes to rejecting uncomfortable projects for officials and Warsaw City Hall under the pretence of non-compliance with documents promoting or programming the development of the capital city of Warsaw. So the clerk knows better what the resident needs. The voting procedure shall then take place. The people vote for projects that they think should be implemented. On the one hand, the citizen's budget was intended to activate the inhabitants in terms of public life, on the other hand, it was intended to help increase the relevance of the investment decisions made by the local authorities. Initially, the interest in the participating budget from the inhabitants of the capital was very high. About 170 thousand took part in the vote. persons. Over time, when it turned out that many uncomfortable projects for the Warsaw authorities were rejected, this interest decreased and today the number of voters was 94 thousand. persons. In addition, the participatory budget has become a tool which officials of Warsaw City Hall and Warsaw Districts use to implement projects that were not within the city or district budget. Another thing that perverts the idea of the civil budget is that there is no possibility to vote against projects that, according to the people, are unnecessary or harmful. An example of this is the project of creating a playground in Dolina Służewska. The project was invented by one of the urban activists and declared as a city-wide initiative. Why is it a city-wide initiative? Because if the people of the district were to vote on it, they probably wouldn't have been supported, because the people didn't agree and they didn't agree to the idea. In Dolina Służewska, which is a place of natural growth of many plants and animals, it was decided to create a playground. Meanwhile, the residents did not want a playground. They wanted to be able to enjoy nature there. Nevertheless, the idea was pushed and the authorities of Warsaw, to the satisfaction of the applicant and to the despair of the inhabitants, simply realized it. The lack of possibility to vote against unwanted projects, the implementation of projects that did not fit into the budget for a given year, the increasing participation of hybrids in voting on projects means that within a few years the idea of the civil budget has been distorted in Warsaw, leading it to caricature in many places. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u714	Statement on commemorating the 83rd anniversary of crystal night. To commemorate the first mass anti-Jewish pogrom in the Third Reich on 9 November in Gliwice, Bytom, Pyskowice, Toszku, Wielkowsi and Zabrze, the memory of those against whom the attackers' actions during crystal night were directed was commemorated. In a universal historiography, this event is referred to as crystal night, the meaning of which comes from fragments of glass and crystals from destroyed sites of Jewish shops and apartments. The initiator of the event commemorating the 83rd anniversary was the Upper Silesian Jewish Memorial House, a branch of the Gliwice Museum. According to historians, the scale and brutality of the actions of the attackers who took part in the crystal night in November 1938 was the announcement of the Holocaust, which was to take place several years later. Under no circumstances can this behavior be identified also by the first manifestation of the anti-Semitic policy of the German authorities, because the economic boycott of the Jews had been introduced accordingly. In Gliwice the meeting place during the celebrations was ul. The average, it's where the plaque commemorates the burnt synagogue is located. The synagogue operated here from 1861 to 1938. It was destroyed by the Nazis on crystal night. I encourage you to familiarize yourself with the historical sources of the Museum in Gliwice, which informs you that more information on this subject can be found by visiting the permanent exhibition in the House of Remembrance of Jews of Górnośląskie at ul. Poniatowskie 14. I would like my statement to be a souvenir of those events, a tribute to the fallen, and a specific statement of opposition to all forms of racism and intolerance, as it was on 9 November that the International Day for Combating Fascism and Anti-Semitism was established.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u716	Declaration by Members on the insufficient financing of the Mountain Voluntary Emergency Service. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Mountain Volunteer Emergency Service, GOPR, is an organization that carries out tasks in the field of mountain rescue. GOPR rescuers run numerous rescue and training activities. It is they who prevent accidents in mountain areas in Poland and help everyone who needs them. The work of GOPR rescuers is extremely difficult and has responsibility. They reach the needy regardless of weather, season, or place, which is often a challenge in mountain areas. GOPR paramedics change full 24-hour shifts all the days of the year in their emergency stations. Today they use modern rescue equipment, which greatly improves their work, such as off-road cars, quads, snowmobiles, avalanche detectors, GPS, radio stations, etc. Thanks to their equipment they are able to help even faster and more efficiently, which significantly affects the safety of mountain tourists. Despite the equipment we have, there are situations where there is not enough, which means that we are unable to provide assistance. In such situations, GOPR rescuers or environments around them themselves organise collections for specialised equipment that are often supported by private individuals. The operation of the Mountain Volunteer Emergency Service is possible thanks to the Ministry of Interior and Administration, but this is not sufficient. As a representative of the deer-legnica land, I would like to draw attention to the growing needs of rescue groups. Everyone who has been in the mountains - whether in the Sudetes or in the Carpathians or in the Świętokrzyskie Mountains - knows how important the work of mountain rescuers is and how important the equipment they have is. The GOPR Foundation organized a fundraiser for the necessary equipment, paying attention to the fact that the time to raise funds is getting less and less, because the winter will soon be in the mountains for good. Skiers, skiers, mountain racers, and dry waltzing will appear. These activities require mountain rescuers specialized equipment so that they can effectively help the victims. GOPR rescuers help and save lives. It is extremely important that they be able to carry this aid effectively, using all necessary equipment.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-11-16-sejm-42-1.u717	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On November 16th, we celebrate International Tolerance Day worldwide. This is a holiday established by the UN General Assembly by a resolution of 12 December 1995. The feast was established on the initiative of UNESCO. The date of this holiday is not random. It was on 16 November 1995 that the UNESCO Member States adopted a declaration on the principles of tolerance. In the declaration we will find among others. A signpost on how we should understand the need for tolerance. It says that tolerance is respect, acceptance and recognition of the richness of the diversity of cultures in the world, our forms of expression and ways of being human. It promotes knowledge, openness, communication and freedom of speech, conscience and faith. Tolerance is harmony in diversity. It is not only a moral obligation, but also a legal and political condition. Tolerance, the value that makes possible peace, contributes to replacing war culture with a culture of peace. Today we especially need tolerance and respect for each other. We need acceptance for diversity, which is a part of each of us. International Tolerance Day can be an opportunity to reflect on this. Reflection on our attitudes towards other people, in particular people and groups particularly vulnerable to social exclusion. This is the reflection of the High House on this particular day I encourage. Thank you.
